Epstein served 13 months of his 18-month sentence before being released on July 22, 2009, and placed on a year of probation on house arrest until August 2010.[[141]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-145)[[142]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-146)[[143]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-147)[[144]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-148) His early release after 13 of 18 months served was because he provided information regarding Bear Stearns executives [Ralph Cioffi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralph_Cioffi "Ralph Cioffi") and [Matthew Tannin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Tannin "Matthew Tannin"),[[145]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-149) whose conduct was scrutinized by the SDNY court in _In re Bear Stearns Companies, Inc. Securities, Derivative and ERISA Litigation_ , before the bank was acquired by [JPMorgan Chase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JPMorgan_Chase "JPMorgan Chase").[[146]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-150)
While on probation, he was allowed numerous trips on his corporate jet to his residences in Manhattan and the U.S. Virgin Islands. He was allowed long shopping trips and walks around Palm Beach "for exercise".[[140]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018b-144) After a contested hearing in January 2011, and an appeal, he stayed registered in New York State as a "level three" (high risk of repeat offense) [sex offender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_offender "Sex offender"), a lifelong designation.[[147]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-151) At that hearing, the Manhattan assistant district attorney, Jennifer Gaffney, argued unsuccessfully that the level should be reduced to a low-risk "level one" and was chided by the judge.[[148]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-152)
#### Reactions
After the accusations against Epstein became public, several people and institutions returned donations that they had received from him, including [Eliot Spitzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliot_Spitzer "Eliot Spitzer"), [Bill Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Richardson "Bill Richardson"),[[149]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Ciarelli-2006-153) and the Palm Beach Police Department.[[117]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Marra-2006-121) [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University "Harvard University") announced it would not return any money.[[149]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Ciarelli-2006-153) Various charitable donations that Epstein had made to finance children's education were also questioned.[[150]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-154)
### Interim developments and media scrutiny
On June 18, 2010, Epstein's former house manager, Alfredo Rodriguez, was sentenced to 18 months' incarceration after being convicted on an obstruction charge for failing to turn over to police, and subsequently trying to sell, a journal in which he had recorded Epstein's activities. FBI special agent Christina Pryor reviewed the material and agreed it was information "that would have been extremely useful in investigating and prosecuting the case, including names and contact information of material witnesses and additional victims."[[151]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-155)[[152]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-156)
Matthew Menchel, the criminal prosecutor at the Miami U.S. Attorney's office, later maintained a personal relationship with Epstein. Menchel had apparently personal meetings with Epstein in 2011, 2012, 2013, and 2017.[[153]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-157)
[Virginia Giuffre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Giuffre "Virginia Giuffre") was among the first of Epstein's accusers to reveal her identity to the public, having done so in 2011.[[154]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-158)[[155]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-159)
####  _Miami Herald_ article
External videos  
---  
[![video icon](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg/20px-Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg) ["Documentary: Who is Jeffrey Epstein, accused of sexually abusing teen girls?"](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1jDPzW9COsU). _Miami Herald_ via YouTube. November 29, 2018.  
In November 2018, [Julie Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_K._Brown "Julie K. Brown") published a three part series in the _[Miami Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Herald "Miami Herald"),_ after she identified 80 victims and located about 60 of them.[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018c-74)[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018a-71)[[140]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018b-144) She quotes the then police chief Reiter as saying "This was 50-something 'shes' and one 'he'—and the 'shes' all basically told the same story."[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018c-74) Brown argued that Epstein had not faced justice due to a plea deal, which she described as a "deal of a lifetime".[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018c-74)
Virginia Roberts Giuffre alleged that Epstein had access to girls through a modelling agency. The article scrutinized Epstein's ties to [Jean-Luc Brunel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_Brunel "Jean-Luc Brunel"), who ran [MC2 model management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MC2_Model_Management "MC2 Model Management"), and scouted models from Brazil and other South American countries, [former Soviet countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Soviet_countries "Former Soviet countries"), and Europe.[[140]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Brown-2018b-144)
Brown's article prompted interest among New York authorities to reopen investigations into Epstein. U.S. Manhattan attorney [Geoffrey Berman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Berman "Geoffrey Berman"), stated "our investigation was assisted by some excellent investigative journalism."[[156]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-160)
#### Sex trafficking charges
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/U.s._v._jeffrey_epstein_indictment_0.pdf/page1-250px-U.s._v._jeffrey_epstein_indictment_0.pdf.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:U.s._v._jeffrey_epstein_indictment_0.pdf)_U.S. v. Jeffrey Epstein_ indictment[[157]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-161)
On July 6, 2019, Epstein was arrested when he returned to the U.S. from France by the [FBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI "FBI")-[NYPD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Police_Department "New York City Police Department") Crimes Against Children Task Force at [Teterboro Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teterboro_Airport "Teterboro Airport") in [New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey "New Jersey") on charges of sex trafficking during the years 2002 to 2005.[[14]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-The_Jerusalem_Post-2019-17)[[158]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-162) He was jailed at the [Metropolitan Correctional Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Correctional_Center,_New_York "Metropolitan Correctional Center, New York") in New York City.[[159]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-163)
Investigators seized Epstein's devices at his homes in New York, Florida and the U.S. Virgin Islands.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) They found CDs, photographs and a videotape containing nude images of females, some who "seemed as if they might be minors".[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164)
According to a memo published four days after his arrest, the search of his New York townhouse turned up evidence of sex trafficking and "hundreds—and perhaps thousands—of sexually suggestive photographs of fully—or partially—nude females." In a locked safe, discs were found with labels: "Young [Name] + [Name]", "Misc nudes 1", and "Girl pics nude". _USA Today_ noted that although investigators were still reviewing the material, one attorney said that his client was underage when some photos were taken.[[161]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Stanglin-2019-165)
FBI investigators later determined that one device contained between 15 and 20 images depicting [child sexual abuse material](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_Sexual_Abuse_Imagery "Child Sexual Abuse Imagery") (CSAM), but that the material had been downloaded from the internet, and not produced by Epstein himself.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) According to a memo later sent to the FBI by U.S. Attorney [Maurene Comey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurene_Comey "Maurene Comey"), "No videos or photos showed Epstein victims being sexually abused, none showed any males with any of the nude females, and none contained evidence implicating anyone other than Epstein and Maxwell". Comey stated they "would have pursued any leads" generated but "we did not, however, locate any such videos".[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164)
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/EFTA00000166_-_Massage_room_with_patterned_wallpaper_a_large_painting_of_a_reclining_figure_and_a_massage_table_-_30th_january_FBI.jpg/250px-EFTA00000166_-_Massage_room_with_patterned_wallpaper_a_large_painting_of_a_reclining_figure_and_a_massage_table_-_30th_january_FBI.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:EFTA00000166_-_Massage_room_with_patterned_wallpaper_a_large_painting_of_a_reclining_figure_and_a_massage_table_-_30th_january_FBI.jpg)Massage room with a [massage table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massage_table "Massage table") in Epstein's NYC townhouse in 2019
Also found in the safe were $70,000 in cash, 48 diamonds,[[162]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Drury-2019-166) and a fraudulent [Austrian passport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_passport "Austrian passport"), which expired in 1987, that had Epstein's photo but another name. The passport had numerous entrance and [exit stamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exit_Stamp "Exit Stamp"), including entrance stamps that showed the use of the passport to enter France, Spain, the United Kingdom, and [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia "Saudi Arabia") in the 1980s. The passport showed his place of residence as Saudi Arabia.[[32]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Winter-2019-35) According to his lawyers, Epstein acquired the fake passport –because as "affluent member of the Jewish faith" he was in danger of being kidnapped abroad – thus it allowed him to conceal his identity. Epstein's lawyers stated and he never used the passport, and the stamps predated his acquisition.[[36]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-AP_and_ToI_Staff-2019-40)
On July 8, prosecutors with the Public Corruption Unit of the [Southern District of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Attorney_for_the_Southern_District_of_New_York "United States Attorney for the Southern District of New York") under [Geoffrey Berman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Berman "Geoffrey Berman") charged him with sex trafficking and conspiracy to traffic minors for sex. The grand jury indictment alleges that "dozens" of[[163]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-167)[[164]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-168)[[165]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-169) Judge [Kenneth Marra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenneth_Marra "Kenneth Marra") was to decide whether the non-prosecution agreement that protected Epstein from the more serious charges should still stand.[[166]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-170)
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Epstein_Bail.png/250px-Epstein_Bail.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Epstein_Bail.png)Federal paperwork regarding Epstein being denied bail
Epstein requested to be released on bond, offering to post $100[[167]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Mangan-2019-171) U.S. district judge [Richard M. Berman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_M._Berman "Richard M. Berman") denied the request on July 18, saying that Epstein posed a danger to the public and a serious flight risk to avoid prosecution.[[167]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Mangan-2019-171) On August 29, 2019, 19 days after Epstein was found dead in his jail cell, the case against Epstein was closed by Judge Berman.[[168]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-172)[[169]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Pierson-2019-173) Prosecutors stated they would continue an investigation for potential co-conspirators.[[169]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Pierson-2019-173)
#### Alleged Acosta comment
On July 9, 2019, the Daily Beast published an article citing an anonymous former White House official, who alleged that during 2017, [Alexander Acosta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Acosta "Alexander Acosta"), the [U.S. Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Attorney "U.S. Attorney") who had overseen Epstein's plea deal, had stated to interviewers of [President Donald Trump's first transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_presidential_transition_of_Donald_Trump "First presidential transition of Donald Trump") team, "I was told Epstein 'belonged to intelligence' and to 'leave it alone'", and that Epstein was "above his [pay grade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pay_grade "Pay grade")".[[68]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Ward-2019-72)[[69]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Schindler-2019-73)
#### Investigation in France
On August 23, 2019, the prosecutor's office in [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris "Paris"), France, opened a preliminary investigation into Epstein, after Yael Mellul wrote to the Paris prosecutor to report of an alleged international [pedophile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedophile "Pedophile") network involving Epstein.[[170]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-174) The investigation aimed to identify potential victims of sexual assault under and over the age of 15, in France and elsewhere against French citizens.[[171]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-175) An associate of Epstein, modeling agent [Jean-Luc Brunel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_Brunel "Jean-Luc Brunel"), was arrested.[[172]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-176) _[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde "Le Monde")_ reported that eleven women accused Brunel of sexual assault in that case, which was closed after he died by suicide in Paris's [La Santé Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Sant%C3%A9_Prison "La Santé Prison"), in February 2022.[[173]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-177)
### Civil cases
Main article: [Litigation involving Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litigation_involving_Jeffrey_Epstein "Litigation involving Jeffrey Epstein")
Epstein faced over a dozen civil lawsuits from accusers and associates while he was living, starting in 2007. Following his death in 2019, more than a dozen additional lawsuits were filed against his estate. 
In October 2007, transgender model [Ava Cordero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ava_Cordero "Ava Cordero") alleged that Epstein had abused her and filed suit accordingly;[[174]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Hansford-2025-178)[[175]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Gorman-2009-179) however, it was dismissed, with press at the time instead making allegations about Cordero's mental health and mocking her gender identity.[[174]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Hansford-2025-178)[[175]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Gorman-2009-179)
#### Later disclosed allegations in lawsuits
Allegations in a civil suit described an occasion where three 12-year-old girls from France were flown in for Epstein's birthday, sexually abused by the financier, and then flown back the following day.[[176]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-180)
## Alleged trafficking ring and blackmail tapes
### Trafficking ring allegations
In 2008, Epstein was convicted in Florida for soliciting a minor and served 13 months. According to police and prosecutors involved in his first criminal investigation, there were no reports from victims that Epstein's high-profile associates were involved.[[177]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Hill-181) In a 2020 [Office of Professional Responsibility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Professional_Responsibility "Office of Professional Responsibility") (OPR) report, federal prosecutor Marie Villafaña said "none of the victims that we spoke with ever talked about any other men being involved in abusing them. It was only Jeffrey Epstein".[[115]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Office_of_Professional_Responsibility-2020-119): 167  According to the OPR report, allegations implicating Epstein's famous contacts emerged years later.[[177]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Hill-181)
In 2009, Virginia Giuffre alleged she was "sexually exploited by Epstein's adult male peers, including royalty" while a minor when working as Epstein's personal masseuse.[[178]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-182) The 2009 suit, filed anonymously, stated that "In addition to being continually exploited to satisfy defendant's [Epstein] every sexual whim, [Ms Giuffre] was also required to be sexually exploited by defendant's adult male peers, including "royalty, politicians, academicians, businessmen and or other professional and personal acquaintances."[[179]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-183) At the time, she named Epstein and Maxwell but did not identify any of the men.[[180]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-184)
In the 2010s, Virginia Giuffre alleged in lawsuits and media interviews, that Epstein had run a sex trafficking ring and "lent out" girls to numerous powerful men in the early 2000s, and directed her to have sex with them.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) She was interviewed by the FBI in 2011 and again in 2019.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) In 2026, documents from Epstein's 2019 investigation were declassified. FBI investigators were unable to corroborate Giuffre's allegation that Epstein was operating a ring in which he "lent out" girls to powerful men. During interviews, numerous women corroborated evidence of Epstein abusing them, but "no other victim has described being expressly directed by either Maxwell or Epstein to engage in sexual activity with other men".[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164)[[181]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-www.justice.gov-2019-185): 59 
When the FBI interviewed two of the victims that Giuffre had claimed were also "lent out", those women denied having such experiences.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) The FBI memo said Giuffre gave "shifting accounts" and "engaged in a continuous stream of public interviews about her allegations, many of which have included sensationalized if not demonstrably inaccurate characterizations of her experiences". It also said Giuffre publicly misrepresented her interactions with the FBI.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164)
The 2019 FBI memo[[181]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-www.justice.gov-2019-185): 60  also states that Giuffre's early draft memoir made mention of Epstein's lawyer [Alan Dershowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Dershowitz "Alan Dershowitz") without allegations of sexual activity, but in 2014, Giuffre raised trafficking allegations.[[182]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Tangalakis-Lippert-2024-186)[[181]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-www.justice.gov-2019-185): 60  Unsealed emails show that in 2011, Giuffre was communicating with _DailyMail_ reporter Sharon Churcher, who she asked to help create a list of Epstein associates to include in her memoir. In one exchange, Churcher said "Don't forget Alan Dershowitz ... JE's buddy and lawyer -good name for your pitch ... We all suspect Alan is a pedo and tho no proof on that, you probably met him when he was hanging out with JE."[[181]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-www.justice.gov-2019-185): 60 [[182]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Tangalakis-Lippert-2024-186) In 2014, Giuffre filed a lawsuit alleging that Epstein had trafficked her to figures including Alan Dershowitz, who she alleged had raped her on 6 occasions.[[183]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-The_Washington_Post-2021-187)[[182]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Tangalakis-Lippert-2024-186) Dershowitz denied her allegations and countersued her, alleging she was fabricating allegations to "extort money" from Epstein associates.[[183]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-The_Washington_Post-2021-187)[[182]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Tangalakis-Lippert-2024-186) In 2022, Giuffre withdrew her legal action against Dershowitz and said she "may have made a mistake" in identifying him.[[184]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-188)[[182]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Tangalakis-Lippert-2024-186)
### Alleged tapes and blackmail operation
Giuffre also alleged that Epstein had recorded her having sex with powerful men for blackmail purposes, and in 2015, accused the FBI of possessing such videos in a [cover-up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cover-up "Cover-up").[[185]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-189) In 2016, [Sarah Ransome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Ransome "Sarah Ransome"), who served as a witness for Giuffre, told a reporter that she had copies of sex tapes filmed by Epstein for blackmail purposes, that included [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump"), [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton"), [Richard Branson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Branson "Richard Branson") and [Andrew Mountbatten-Windsor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Mountbatten-Windsor "Andrew Mountbatten-Windsor"). However, Ransome admitted to fabricating her claim in 2019.[[186]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-190)[[187]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-191)
Following Epstein's death in August 2019, reports of there being cameras in his homes circulated in the media. _Vanity Fair_ reported that Ghislaine Maxwell said that [Epstein's island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein%27s_island "Epstein's island") was "completely wired" with cameras; a friend speculated this was for blackmail purposes.[[188]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-192) When police raided [his Palm Beach residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein%27s_Palm_Beach_mansion "Jeffrey Epstein's Palm Beach mansion") in 2006, two hidden cameras were discovered in his home.[[189]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-193) In November 2019, [Maria Farmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Farmer "Maria Farmer"), an artist who worked for Epstein in 1996, said that Epstein had shown her a hidden room in his [New York mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_N._Straus_House "Herbert N. Straus House") where he had teams of men monitoring rooms in the home using "pinhole cameras", including the bedrooms and toilets.[[190]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-194) Epstein implied that he had blackmail material when he told a _New York Times_ reporter in 2018, off the record, that he held incriminating information about powerful people, including information about their sexual proclivities and recreational drug use.[[191]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Stewart_2019b-195) In July 2021, _Rolling Stone_ reported that multiple sources, including former arms dealers and former intelligence figures, suggested that Epstein recorded influential individuals in compromising situations.[[192]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-196) In 2022, Courtney Soerensen alleged that in 1988, Epstein sexually groped her in France while a videographer filmed the encounter.[[193]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-six-197) In 2025, Marina Lacerda, an alleged minor victim recruited by Epstein in 2002, described his New York home as being full of cameras with an office in the front of the house dedicated to them.[[194]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-198)
Federal investigators seized Epstein's devices during 2019 raids on his homes.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) Authorities found CDs, photographs and tapes, some labelled "young [name] + [name]",[[161]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Stanglin-2019-165) and depicting girls who "seemed as if they might be minors".[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164) The FBI investigation, made public in 2026, found that none of the photos or videos depicted victims being sexually abused by Epstein or others. Investigators found between 15 and 20 images depicting [child sexual abuse material](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_Sexual_Abuse_Imagery "Child Sexual Abuse Imagery") (CSAM), but they determined that these were downloaded from the internet, not produced by Epstein.[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Sisak-2026-164)
In 2019, two prominent lawyers representing Epstein's accusers, [David Boies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Boies "David Boies") and [J. Stanley Pottinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Stanley_Pottinger "J. Stanley Pottinger"), were contacted by a man purporting to be a hacker, who went by the pseudonym Patrick Kessler. Kessler claimed to possess sex tapes of Epstein and high profile men with victims, and told the lawyers he wanted the men to face justice.[[195]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:1-199) In message exchanges, Pottinger instead proposed using the videos to extract large financial settlements from the men and keeping the tapes private. In other exchanges, he "described hypothetical plans in which the lawyers would pocket up to 40 percent of the settlements and could extract money from wealthy men by flipping from representing victims to representing their alleged abusers". The tapes, however, did not exist. Kessler had constructed an elaborate story, and the messages were then provided to the _New York Times._[[195]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:1-199)[[196]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:2-200) Boies denied involvement, but declined to condemn Pottinger.[[196]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:2-200)
## Alleged adult victims
Following Epstein's 2008 conviction, Epstein reportedly began pursuing relationships with young adult women over the age of 18. According to victims lawyer [Brad Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_J._Edwards "Bradley J. Edwards"), Epstein "deliberately shifted to women over 18 to use their age as cover" for what he argues was a sex trafficking operation.[[197]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:0-201) Many of the adult women Epstein pursued were fashion models from Europe and [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia "Russia").[[197]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:0-201)
Emails released in the Epstein Files identified one such woman as Svetlana Pozhidaeva, who, while an adult, began working an assistant to the sex offender from 2008 until his death in 2019. Previously unredacted emails showed she was gathering information on young women and models, and sending it to Epstein. Her name has since been redacted by the Department of Justice. According to lawyers speaking to the _[Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal "The Wall Street Journal"),_ Pozhidaeva is an example of an adult victim, who was coerced into assisting Epstein's trafficking. She is represented by a major Epstein victims lawyer, [Brad Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_J._Edwards "Bradley J. Edwards"), and denies allegations that she or her father were Russian spies.[[197]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:0-201)
According to Safdar, redaction failures of emails in the files gave "ammunition" to online commentators, who cited friendly, warm or grateful messages between adult women and Epstein as evidence they were "willing participants", or as "evidence of complicity or consent". The lawyer Moira Penza argued against the idea that adult women "could have just left" Epstein, as she believes co-dependency means "consent just becomes irrelevant. There really is no way to consent".[[197]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:0-201)
In 2025, a woman of Russian origin filed a class action lawsuit against [Bank of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_America "Bank of America"), alleging that while an adult,[[198]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:4-202) the bank had enabled Epstein to traffick and control her life between 2011 until 2019.[[199]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Neumeister-203) As of March 2026, the lawsuit has been tentatively settled.[[199]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Neumeister-203)
In the _Wall Street Journal_ , [Michael Tracey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Tracey_\(journalist\) "Michael Tracey \(journalist\)") argued that "excessive redactions" in Epstein files releases made the number of alleged adult victims unclear, writing: "as far as I can ascertain based on publicly available information, a significant majority of purported Epstein victims were adults at the time of their claimed victimization, not vulnerable children—an inconvenient detail that the dissemination of unredacted records would likely illuminate".[[198]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:4-202)
## Personal life
Main article: [Personal life of Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_life_of_Jeffrey_Epstein "Personal life of Jeffrey Epstein")
Further information: [Connections of Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connections_of_Jeffrey_Epstein "Connections of Jeffrey Epstein") and [List of people named in the Epstein files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_named_in_the_Epstein_files "List of people named in the Epstein files")
In 2003, _Bloomberg_ journalist David Bank spoke on Little St. James with Epstein in a 5-hour long interview, which Bank left unpublished prior to Epstein's death.[[200]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-204)[[201]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-205)[[202]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-206) In 2017, Epstein spoke in interviews, over the course of more than one hundred hours, with journalist [Michael Wolff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Wolff_\(journalist\) "Michael Wolff \(journalist\)"), which began to be released in November 2024, as part of Wolff's _Fire and Fury_ [podcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podcast "Podcast").[[203]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-207)[[204]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-208)[[205]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-209)
## Death
Main article: [Death of Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Jeffrey_Epstein "Death of Jeffrey Epstein")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/MCC_New_York_jeh.JPG/250px-MCC_New_York_jeh.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:MCC_New_York_jeh.JPG)At the time of his death, Epstein was being held at the [Metropolitan Correctional Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Correctional_Center,_New_York "Metropolitan Correctional Center, New York"), awaiting trial for sex trafficking.[[206]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Dienst-2019-210)
On July 23, 2019, Epstein was found injured and semiconscious at 1:30[EDT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Time_Zone "Eastern Time Zone") on the floor of his cell at the [Metropolitan Correctional Center, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Correctional_Center,_New_York "Metropolitan Correctional Center, New York") (MCC), with marks around his neck.[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211)[[208]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Rashbaum-2019-212)[[209]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-213)[[210]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-214)[[211]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-215) His cellmate, [Nicholas Tartaglione](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Tartaglione "Nicholas Tartaglione"), was questioned about Epstein's condition. Tartaglione denied having any knowledge of what happened. Correctional staff suspected attempted suicide, but did not rule out the possibility it was staged or assault by another inmate.[[206]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Dienst-2019-210)[[212]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Leonnig-2019-216)[[162]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Drury-2019-166)[[213]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-217)[[208]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Rashbaum-2019-212) After that incident, he was placed on [suicide watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_watch "Suicide watch").[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211)[[214]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-218)[[215]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-219)[[216]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-220) Six days later, on July 29, 2019, Epstein was taken off suicide watch and placed in a special housing unit with another inmate.[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211)
When Epstein was placed in the special housing unit, the jail informed the Justice Department that he would have a cellmate, and that a guard would look into the cell every 30 minutes. These procedures were not followed on the night of his death.[[217]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019a-221)[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211)[[218]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Benner-2019-222) On August 9, 2019, Epstein's cellmate was transferred out, but no one took his place.[[219]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-223) Later in the evening, contrary to the jail's normal procedure, Epstein was not checked every 30 minutes.[[217]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019a-221)[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211)[[218]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Benner-2019-222) The two guards assigned to check his unit that night fell asleep and did not check on him for three hours; the guards falsified related records.[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211)[[220]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-224) The two cameras in front of Epstein's cell were claimed to have malfunctioned that night.[[221]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Hosenball-2019-225)
Epstein was found dead in his cell at 6:30[[222]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-226)[[223]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-227) Emergency responders were called and he was taken to a hospital. On August 10, the [Bureau of Prisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Bureau_of_Prisons "Federal Bureau of Prisons") and U.S. attorney general [William Barr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Barr "William Barr") called the death an apparent suicide, although no final determination had been made.[[224]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Zapotosky-2019b-228) The [U.S. Department of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_Justice "U.S. Department of Justice")'s Inspector General's investigation report released in 2023, criticized jail officials for repeated "negligence, misconduct, and outright job performance failures". It denied the suggestion that what happened was anything other than a suicide.[[225]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-229) In May 2025, the FBI Deputy Director [Dan Bongino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Bongino "Dan Bongino") stated that the surveillance footage from the night of Epstein's death shows Epstein alone in his cell, with no evidence of outside involvement, reaffirming the official ruling of suicide.[[226]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-230)
### Autopsy
[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Epstein_Body_Moved_From_New_York_Hospital_to_Medical_Examiner%27s_Office.webm "Play video")[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Epstein_Body_Moved_From_New_York_Hospital_to_Medical_Examiner%27s_Office.webm)Epstein's body moved from New York hospital to medical examiner's office
On August 11, 2019, an [autopsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autopsy "Autopsy") was performed.[[227]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-231) It appeared likely that Epstein had thrown himself violently off the cell's top bunk, which would explain the damage he suffered, other than strangulation.[[228]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-232) The preliminary result of the autopsy found that Epstein sustained breaks in his neck bones, including the [hyoid bone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyoid_bone "Hyoid bone"). Such breaks of the hyoid bone can occur from those who hang themselves from a substantial height, e.g. jumping from a chair into the rope, but are more common in victims of strangulation.[[229]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-233)
On August 16, 2019, [Barbara Sampson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Sampson "Barbara Sampson"), the [New York City medical examiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_medical_examiner "New York City medical examiner"), ruled Epstein's death a [suicide by hanging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_by_hanging "Suicide by hanging").[[230]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-234) The medical examiner, according to Epstein's defense counsel, only saw nine minutes of footage from one security camera to help her arrive at her conclusion.[[231]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Stockler-2019-235) Epstein's defense lawyers were not satisfied with the conclusion and said the evidence concerning Epstein's death was "far more consistent" with murder.[[231]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Stockler-2019-235)[[232]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-236) [Michael Baden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Baden "Michael Baden"), an independent pathologist hired by the Epstein estate, observed the autopsy. In October 2019, Baden said that Epstein had experienced a number of injuries—among them a broken bone in his neck—that "are extremely unusual in suicidal hangings and could occur much more commonly in homicidal strangulation."[[233]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-237)
### Final will
On August 18, 2019, it was reported that Epstein had signed his [last will and testament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_will_and_testament "Last will and testament") on August 8, two weeks after being found injured in his cell and two days before his death.[[206]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Dienst-2019-210) Until this time, Epstein had been depositing money in other inmates' [commissary accounts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prison_commissary "Prison commissary") to avoid being attacked.[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Watkins-2019b-211) Epstein's will left $50[Richard Kahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Kahn_\(accountant\) "Richard Kahn \(accountant\)"), $10[Ghislaine Maxwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghislaine_Maxwell "Ghislaine Maxwell"), and $10[[234]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-238) The trust's enforceability remained uncertain.[[235]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-239)[[236]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-240) As of February 2026[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Jeffrey_Epstein&action=edit), Kahn and Indyke control [Epstein's estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein%27s_estate "Epstein's estate").[[237]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Goldstein-2026-241) Due to ongoing lawsuits and victim settlements, Epstein's beneficiaries will only receive a share of what is left after these are resolved.[[238]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-242)[[237]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Goldstein-2026-241)
### Burial
Following the autopsy, Epstein's body was claimed by his brother Mark.[[239]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-243)[[240]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-244) On September 5, 2019, Epstein's body was interred in an unmarked crypt next to those of his parents at the I.J. Morris Star of David Cemetery in [Palm Beach, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Beach,_Florida "Palm Beach, Florida"). The names of his parents were removed to prevent vandalism.[[241]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-245)
### Investigations
[![Wikimedia Commons logo](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/4a/Commons-logo.svg/40px-Commons-logo.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Commons-logo.svg)
Wikimedia Commons has media related to [Epstein Files: First Phase](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Category:Epstein_files_-_phase_1 "commons:Category:Epstein files - phase 1").
Attorney General Barr ordered an investigation by the [U.S. Department of Justice Office of the Inspector General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_Justice_Office_of_the_Inspector_General "U.S. Department of Justice Office of the Inspector General") in addition to the FBI investigation, saying he was "appalled" by Epstein's death in federal custody.[[224]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Zapotosky-2019b-228)[[242]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-246) Two days later Barr said there had been "serious irregularities" in the prison's handling of Epstein, promising "there will be accountability."[[243]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-247)
[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jeffrey_Epstein%27s_cell_block_CCTV_footage_\(10hr\)_released_by_the_US_Department_of_Justice_in_July_2025.webm "Play video")[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jeffrey_Epstein%27s_cell_block_CCTV_footage_\(10hr\)_released_by_the_US_Department_of_Justice_in_July_2025.webm)CCTV footage released in July 2025 showing part of a common area within the Metropolitan Correctional Center where Epstein was being held. A minute is missing from the footage, where the clock jumps from 11:58:58 to 12:00:00.
The president of the Council of Prison Locals C-33 E. Young, stated that prisons "can't ever stop anyone who is persistent on killing themselves."[[244]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Zapotosky-2019a-248) The previous inmate suicide in that MCC facility was in 1998.[[245]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-249) Union leader Young said it was unclear if there was video of Epstein's hanging or direct observations by jail officials. He said that while cameras are ubiquitous, he did not believe the interior of inmates' cells was within their range.[[244]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Zapotosky-2019a-248)
[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Trump_and_Pam_Bondi_on_Jeffrey_Epstein,_July_8th_2025.webm "Play video")[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Trump_and_Pam_Bondi_on_Jeffrey_Epstein,_July_8th_2025.webm)President Trump and Attorney General [Pam Bondi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pam_Bondi "Pam Bondi") briefly mentioning Epstein during a cabinet meeting on July 8, 2025.
Epstein's attorneys asked district judge [Richard M. Berman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_M._Berman "Richard M. Berman"), to probe the death, alleging they could provide evidence the incident resulting in his death was "far more consistent with assault" than suicide.[[231]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Stockler-2019-235) At least one camera in the hallway outside Epstein's cell had footage that was unusable, though other usable footage was recorded in the area.[[212]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Leonnig-2019-216) Two cameras that malfunctioned in front of Epstein's cell were sent to an [FBI lab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI_Laboratory "FBI Laboratory") for examination.[[221]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Hosenball-2019-225) Federal prosecutors subpoenaed 20 correctional officers concerning the cause of Epstein's death.[[246]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-250)
On November 19, prosecutors charged guards Michael Thomas and Tova Noel with creating false records, and conspiracy, after footage revealed that Epstein had, against regulation, been in his cell unchecked for eight hours prior to being found dead.[[247]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-251)[[248]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-252)[[249]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-253) On May 22, 2021, the guards admitted they falsified records but were spared jail time under a deal.[[250]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-254) On May 25, they plead guilty to falsifying records and conspiracy to defraud the U.S. They were sentenced to six months supervisory release and 100 hours community service.[[251]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-255)
In December 2023, judge [Loretta Preska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loretta_Preska "Loretta Preska") ordered a list with names of 170 Epstein associates to be unsealed on January 1, 2024.[[252]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-256) Anyone on the list had until January 1 to appeal to have their name removed.[[253]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-257)[[254]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-258)[[255]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-259)[[256]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-260)[[257]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-261) In February 2025, the [second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump "Second presidency of Donald Trump")'s attorney general [Pam Bondi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pam_Bondi "Pam Bondi") stated that Epstein's [client list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein_client_list "Jeffrey Epstein client list") was "sitting on my desk" for review, and in June, [Elon Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Musk "Elon Musk") alleged Trump was in the files. In July, Bondi and FBI head [Kash Patel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kash_Patel "Kash Patel") announced there was no list, no evidence Epstein had blackmailed anyone, that Epstein had killed himself, and released footage showing a partial view of a common area and obscured view of the stairs leading to Epstein's cell block—though that footage was not independently verified.[[258]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-262)[[259]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-263)[[260]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-264)[[261]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-265) A minute was found missing, where the clock jumps from 11:58:58 to 12:00:00.[[262]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-266)[[263]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-267) An investigation found the video had been modified despite the FBI claim it was raw,[[264]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-268) and three minutes were cut out of the video.[[265]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-269) On July 15, Representative [Thomas Massie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Massie "Thomas Massie") submitted [House Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Resolution "House Resolution") 119–581, co-sponsored by [Ro Khanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ro_Khanna "Ro Khanna"), to force the Department of Justice to release the Epstein files.[[266]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-270)[[267]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-271)[[268]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-272) They announced on August 12 they would bring victims to the Capitol on September 3.[[269]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-273)[[270]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-274)
On August 25, the [House Oversight Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Oversight_Committee "House Oversight Committee") issued [subpoenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subpoena "Subpoena") to [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton"), [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton") and former attorneys general [Loretta Lynch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loretta_Lynch "Loretta Lynch"), [Eric Holder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Holder "Eric Holder"), [William Barr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Barr "William Barr"), [Merrick Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrick_Garland "Merrick Garland"), [Jeff Sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Sessions "Jeff Sessions"), and [Alberto Gonzales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Gonzales "Alberto Gonzales"). Acosta testified in October about his plea deal with Epstein, where he defended his decision to pursue a state-level case due to perceived weaknesses in federal evidence, and denied knowledge of Epstein's extensive financial crimes. These claims were disputed by Democrats, highlighting contradictions with later evidence of his office investigating financial aspects and calling his testimony non-credible.[[271]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-275)
On September 4, [James O'Keefe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_O%27Keefe "James O'Keefe") published an exposé on Deputy Chief Joseph Schnitt of DOJ Special Operations, in which the latter said there were "thousands and thousands of pages of files" relating to Epstein and the DOJ would "redact every Republican or conservative person in those files", while "[leaving] all the liberal, Democratic people in those files".[[272]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-276)[[273]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-277)[[274]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-278)[[275]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-279) Epstein's brother Mark alleged the same, stating he had heard from a "good source" that a team in Virginia was "sanitizing" and "scrubbing the files to take Republican names out."[[276]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-280) On November 12, [Adelita Grijalva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adelita_Grijalva "Adelita Grijalva") gave the final 218th signature to Massie's discharge position, forcing the creation of the [Epstein Files Transparency Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein_Files_Transparency_Act "Epstein Files Transparency Act"). It requires the attorney general to "make publicly available" all files pertaining to the prosecution of Epstein within 30 days, and give the Judiciary Committees an unredacted "list of all government officials and politically exposed persons" named in the files.[[277]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-281)
## Estate and victim settlements
### Epstein estate settlements
Main article: [Estate of Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_of_Jeffrey_Epstein "Estate of Jeffrey Epstein")
Epstein's estate was valued around $600 million at the time of his death.[[237]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Goldstein-2026-241) His estate has become the focus of extensive litigation and settlements. Epstein's estate created a victims' compensation program that paid about $121 million to 136 claimants.[[278]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Breuninger-2022-282) The estate later paid an additional $49 million to settle a victims lawsuit.[[279]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-283) In 2026, the estate paid $35 million to settle another victims lawsuit.[[280]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-284) In 2022, the estate also settled a lawsuit with the government of the U.S. Virgin Islands to resolve civil claims that Epstein used the territory for trafficking, paying $105 million and agreeing to share proceeds from the sale of his private islands.[[278]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Breuninger-2022-282)
### Bank lawsuits
In November 2023, JPMorgan Chase agreed to pay a settlement of $290 million to resolve a class-action lawsuit brought by Epstein accusers, who alleged the bank enabled his abuse by continuing to provide him their financial services.[[281]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Goldstein-2023-285) JP Morgan did not admit liability as part of the settlement.[[281]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-Goldstein-2023-285) The lawyers received 30% of the settlement.[[282]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-286)
Around the same time, Deutsche Bank agreed to pay $75 million to settle a similar lawsuit brought by Epstein accusers concerning the period when Epstein was a client of the bank, between 2013 and 2018.[[283]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-287) JPMorgan later paid $75 million to settle claims by the government of the U.S. Virgin Islands related to allegations that the bank had enabled Epstein's trafficking.[[284]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-288)
In 2025, victim lawsuits were also filed against [Bank of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_America "Bank of America") and the [Bank of New York Mellon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BNY "BNY") (BNY), alleging these banks enabled Epsteins trafficking. Both banks said they would fight the lawsuits, with BNY describing the lawsuit as "meritless".[[285]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-289) In February 2026, BNY won a dismissal of all claims in the lawsuit.[[286]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:3-290) 4 out of 6 claims against Bank of America were dismissed, with two proceeding.[[286]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-:3-290)
## Epstein files releases
Main articles: [Epstein files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein_files "Epstein files") and [Jeffrey Epstein's birthday book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein%27s_birthday_book "Jeffrey Epstein's birthday book")
The Epstein files are a U.S. government release of millions of documents pertaining to investigations of Epstein. 
In September 2025, the [House Oversight Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Oversight_and_Accountability "United States House Committee on Oversight and Accountability") released a 2003 birthday album created for Epstein's 50th birthday, titled _[The First Fifty Years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein%27s_birthday_book "Jeffrey Epstein's birthday book")_. The album contained letters and drawings from various associates.[[287]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-291) One letter in the collection was attributed to [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump"), although Trump has denied writing or signing it and his legal team has challenged its authenticity.[[288]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-292)
## Legacy
Further information: [Epstein didn't kill himself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein_didn%27t_kill_himself "Epstein didn't kill himself") and [Epstein files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein_files "Epstein files")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Epstein_Bridge_Nov2019_2_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Epstein_Bridge_Nov2019_2_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Epstein_Bridge_Nov2019_2_\(cropped\).jpg)Graffiti featuring the phrase on an overpass on [Interstate 71](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_71 "Interstate 71") in [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati "Cincinnati")
### Public reaction and institutional scrutiny
Epstein's death in 2019 fueled widespread controversy and debate. The belief that he was the victim of murder in service of a wider cover-up subsequently became a popular [internet meme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_meme "Internet meme") and conspiracy theory.[[289]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-293)[[290]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-294)
The partial release of the [Epstein files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein_files "Epstein files") by the U.S. Department of Justice in response to the [Epstein Files Transparency Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epstein_Files_Transparency_Act "Epstein Files Transparency Act") prompted renewed public and media scrutiny of the case and contributed to a marked shift in public discourse about [elite accountability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accountability "Accountability").[[291]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-295)[[292]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-296)[[293]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-297) Legal scholars, investigative journalists, and human rights experts described the disclosures as intensifying scrutiny of longstanding [institutional failures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_decay "Political decay") which had allowed Epstein to avoid accountability, despite repeated allegations.[[294]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-298)[[295]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-299) Commentators noted that claims concerning Epstein's extensive connections within the political, financial, and social [elite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elite "Elite")–which had long been dismissed by many officials and media figures as speculative or unsubstantiated–were reassessed following the document release.[[296]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-300) The case has been frequently cited as illustrative of broader [class-based disparities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_inequality "Social inequality") in law enforcement and prosecutorial decision-making, and as an example of systemic [corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_the_United_States "Corruption in the United States") and elite impunity within the [criminal justice system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_justice "Criminal justice"), particularly regarding the treatment of [wealthy or well-connected individuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_class "Upper class").[[297]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-301)
### Artworks
On July 1, 2020, a statue of Epstein was left outside the City Hall in [Albuquerque, New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque,_New_Mexico "Albuquerque, New Mexico") as a satirical commentary on opposition to the [removal of Confederate monuments and memorials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Removal_of_Confederate_monuments_and_memorials "Removal of Confederate monuments and memorials").[[298]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-302)[[299]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-303) A sculpture of Epstein frolicking with Trump titled _[Best Friends Forever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Friends_Forever_\(sculpture\) "Best Friends Forever \(sculpture\)")_ was produced by an anonymous art group aliased "The Secret Handshake" in protest of their relationship. Its September 2025 debut at the [National Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Mall "National Mall") made national news when the [United States Park Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Park_Police "United States Park Police") assigned with protecting the sculpture dismantled it.[[300]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-304)
### Documentaries
As of October 2019, [HBO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO "HBO") was creating a limited series on Epstein's life and death to be directed and executive produced by [Adam McKay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_McKay "Adam McKay").[[301]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-305)[[302]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-306) [Sony Pictures Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_Pictures_Television "Sony Pictures Television") is additionally developing a miniseries based on Epstein's life.[[303]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-307) The [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix") documentary series _[Jeffrey Epstein: Filthy Rich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein:_Filthy_Rich "Jeffrey Epstein: Filthy Rich")_ premiered in May 2020.[[304]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-308) The [Lifetime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lifetime_\(TV_network\) "Lifetime \(TV network\)") documentary _[Surviving Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surviving_Jeffrey_Epstein "Surviving Jeffrey Epstein")_ premiered in August 2020.[[305]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein#cite_note-309)
